<a id="node-0490"></a>
### NODE-0490 · Place copied layouts at the cursor

**2026-11-11 · Changed · Implemented · Local**

Ctrl or Cmd+V previews the copied layout at the cursor. Click an open position to place it, or press Esc or right-click to cancel.

- The preview preserves relative spacing and internal cables. A blocked click keeps the preview active. The clipboard remains available after placement, cancellation, and travel between map nodes.

Topics: Interface · Controls · World · Documentation

Supersedes: [NODE-0488](<2026-11.md#node-0488>)

Verification: A local browser fixture dispatched Copy and Paste key events through the game handler. A blocked click showed a red two-node preview, an open click placed both nodes together, and Escape canceled a second preview. Engine tests covered exact placement, blocked positions, cross-map clipboard reuse, internal cables, control groups, and Undo. The production build and 1,736 tests passed, with two tests skipped.

<a id="node-0488"></a>
### NODE-0488 · Copy and paste selected nodes across map nodes

**2026-11-11 · Changed · Superseded · Local**

Ctrl or Cmd+C copies the selected layout. Ctrl or Cmd+V pastes it near the center of the current view, including after traveling to another map node.

- The in-game clipboard retains layout, configurations, internal cables and complete control groups during the session. Pasted machines start empty, and each paste can be undone.

Topics: Interface · Controls · World · Documentation

Replaced by: [NODE-0490](<2026-11.md#node-0490>)

Verification: A local browser fixture dispatched shortcut key events through the game handler. Copy left Home unchanged. After traveling to E1, two pastes added one node each and Undo removed the second. The build passed, and 125 focused tests covered the engine, control groups, and cross-map layout and cable snapshots.

<a id="node-0486"></a>
### NODE-0486 · Show the active Recycler recipe and give Extractors turns at shared inputs

**2026-11-11 · Changed · Implemented · Local**

The Recycler always chooses its recipe from the incoming feed. Its recipe panel shows the active recipe first and indicates when it is waiting for Water. When Extractors compete for an available input slot, the longest-waiting eligible Extractor sends first.

- The recipe panel is a reference. Formerly pinned Recyclers switch automatically after their current material and recovered output clear. Each batch uses its dedicated Water input.

Topics: Production · Recipes · Connections · Interface

Supersedes: [NODE-0477](<2026-11.md#node-0477>)

Verification: Inspected the W1 playtest factory. An isolated browser fixture showed Recycle Stone first, showed Waiting for Water with two buffered Stone, and cleared both Stone and the active card after Water was connected. Engine tests cover a formerly pinned recipe and three Extractors sharing capacity through chained Joints. The production build and 1,732 tests passed, with two tests skipped.

<a id="node-0485"></a>
### NODE-0485 · Clear automatic Recycler disposal after the material drains

**2026-11-11 · Fixed · Implemented · Local**

Raw resource disposal processes each batch without needing output space. The automatic Recycler clears the disposal recipe and restores its output socket when the batch, material buffer, and incoming material are clear.

- Each disposal batch uses its resource and one Water. Any recovered stock already in the Recycler retains a usable output socket.

Topics: Production · Recipes · Connections · Interface

Verification: Engine regressions cover ordinary cable feeds, multiple raw items, excess saved output, and restoration of the output socket after automatic disposal. The production build and 1,731 tests passed, with two tests skipped.

### NODE-0482 · Alert on resource depletion and scale minimap icons with zoom

**2026-11-11 · Changed · Implemented · Local**

An exhausted finite reserve triggers a red notification naming the resource and map node. The tile pulses red on both maps until the player enters it. Minimap icons grow with their tiles, and the base settlement is named Goblin Camp.

- The marker persists through save and reload, resource regeneration, and ordinary undo. Reduced-motion mode uses a steady red highlight. Newly generated camps and old saved default names both use Goblin Camp.

Topics: Interface · World · Encounters · Production · Persistence · Documentation

Verification: An isolated browser factory depleted Copper at E1, displayed the centered warning, kept the pulse after the toast ended and while selecting the tile, and cleared it on travel. Verified the Goblin Camp tooltip and proportional marker sizing at 100% and 160% zoom. Engine tests cover a collapsed deposit, remote Forest refill, event churn, save reload, travel and undo. The production build and 1,730 tests passed, with two tests skipped.

### NODE-0477 · Match Recycler recipes to the material feed

**2026-11-11 · Changed · Superseded · Local**

Place and wire a Recycler before choosing a recipe. The first material dispatched selects its unlocked recipe, while Water enters through its own socket.

- An automatic Recycler stays on one recipe until the batch, material in transit, material buffer and output are clear. Water stock carries across the switch. The picker can pin a recipe or resume matching incoming material, and manually configured machines retain their selection.

Topics: Production · Recipes · Connections · Interface · Documentation

Replaced by: [NODE-0486](<2026-11.md#node-0486>)

Verification: Verified in an isolated local browser by wiring an Extractor into an unconfigured Recycler and checking that Recycle Copper was selected automatically. Tests cover Water-first and crossed sockets, locked waste, mixed-feed hold, and manual pinning. Build passed and Vitest reported 1,726 passed with two skips. Reviewed the four updated Word documents.

<a id="node-0476"></a>
### NODE-0476 · Simulate direct Iron and Copper Plate recipes

**2026-11-11 · Review · Recorded · Not applicable**

Compare an eight-second Synthesizer shortcut with Kiln and Smelter production. The shortcut simplifies lakeside wiring, while shipping ore from a dry outpost uses two freight items per delivered plate.

- At rank 2 the lakeside routes delivered 12.5 and 9.4 plates per minute per metal. The dry outpost delivered 12 plates per minute per metal by shipping plates on Carts and 6 by shipping ore for direct processing. Trucks restored direct production to 9.4. With one base ore Extractor per metal both lakeside routes sustained 6.
- The shared base Pump supported both direct Plate recipes plus 20 Coolant and 15 Copper Slurry per minute with staged solid inputs. The dry local Synthesizers delivered zero plates without Water.

Topics: Production · Recipes · Logistics · Playtesting

Verification: Ran 15 scenarios through the actual engine in 100 ms steps for 12 game minutes each. Two runs produced byte-identical reports. Used prebuilt fixtures, ordinary connections, extraction and Roads, and in-memory prototype recipes. The report excludes player setup, travel, and unlock time.

<a id="node-0474"></a>
### NODE-0474 · Move Mythril recipes and configure Recyclers one recipe at a time

**2026-11-10 · Changed · Implemented · Local**

Both Mythril Mesh recipes and both composite Mythril recipes now belong to the Synthesizer. Crust Recovery moves to the Recycler, where every unit runs one selected recipe using a dedicated Water inlet.

- The Mesh recipes retain Fabricator efficiency, the composites retain Assembler efficiency, and Crust Recovery retains Synthesizer and Mineral Recovery bonuses. Select Crust Recovery after Intermediate Recycling and Efficient Higher Intermediates.
- The Recycler picker groups waste recovery and breakdown. The selected recipe determines the material socket, and concurrent recovery streams require separate machines. Existing automatic Recyclers need a recipe selection. The proposal records Hydro Plant socket labels and normal-play routing checks as follow-up work.

Topics: Production · Recipes · Research · Interface · Documentation

Supersedes: [NODE-0470](<2026-11.md#node-0470>)

Verification: The production build and full Vitest suite passed with 1,723 tests and two skips. Tests verify moved recipe availability, former-producer rejection, actual and displayed timings, crossed socket rejection, output blocking, and mixed-strength Suppression Bot recycling. An isolated local browser verified the recipe picker, Crust selection, Water and Crust socket labels, and the No output display. Verified 126 chart rows against the game catalog and reviewed all four rendered Word documents.

### NODE-0473 · Simulate the proposed Synthesizer recipe transfers

**2026-11-10 · Review · Recorded · Not applicable**

All four proposed transfers produced their expected outputs with ordinary cables in a headless engine fixture. Holding effective cycle speeds fixed preserved modeled materials, bot rates and time to produce encounter AP.

- With origin machines at rank 2 and the Synthesizer at rank 0, the four batches took 25% longer and the modeled first Shiny Core moved from 136.6 to 140.2 seconds. <a id="node-0471"></a>
### NODE-0471 · Require Water for every Recycler recipe

**2026-11-10 · Changed · Implemented · Local**

Every Recycler batch uses one Water from its dedicated inlet, including crafted-item breakdown and raw-resource disposal. The node indicates when it is waiting for Water.

### NODE-0446 · Combine Lake Intake and Pump

**2026-11-09 · Changed · Implemented · Published by afdea7a**

Water Tools unlocks a single Production Pump. A direct Lake cable draws Water, while a fluid feed from another node relays Water or Coolant to available outlets.

- Each Lake supports two Pumps. Base extraction is 4 Water per second, rising to 8 with Pressure Systems and 16 with High Pressure Intakes.
- The input cable determines the mode. The Mods editor lists Pump under Production.

### NODE-0438 · Use one field size across the world and align paired Roads

**2026-11-09 · Changed · Implemented · Published by afdea7a**

Start each sector at 3,600 × 2,400 in new worlds and grow the entire world together through Area Expansion. Roads use matching positions along the shared wall and show the neighboring ghost while dragged.

- Crowded new sectors retry their seeded terrain layout. Existing Road pairs align on load or expansion when both locations and cable routes are clear. During drag, both endpoints slide on their assigned walls and the ghost shows the receiving cables.

**2026-11-09 · Changed · Superseded · Local**

Connect a Cart, Truck, or Train directly to a sending Road. Place any Filters, Splitters, Mergers, or Joints before the freight vehicle. The receiving Road can unload into compatible local nodes.

- Freight vehicle is the shared term for Carts, Trucks, and Trains. The Road flow panel, cable feedback, build and research descriptions, and Expansion tutorial explain the direct connection rule.
- Existing invalid sending cables stay visible and idle until rewired. Cargo stays with its source. Legacy Road cargo recovery and the vehicle upgrade path are retained. The earlier records are superseded only for direct Dev Source and other non-vehicle Road feeds, including sending-side router chains.
- The stress fixture has a Cart at every sending Road. The offline endgame harness can stage freight at a Cart near a Road when the original source needs a detour.

<a id="node-0384"></a>
### NODE-0384 · Reset Map Node prices when a Core tier enters

**2026-11-08 · Changed · Implemented · Published by 19dc40f**

Start each new Core tier at five of each required Core and ramp evenly over ten purchases to the existing tier ceiling.

- Keep the Basic, Automata, Hydro, Shiny and Complex ceilings at 10, 15, 20, 25 and 30 each. On schedule, opening 61 maps costs 2,797 Cores, including Exploration. All 121 maps cost 15,937 Cores.
- If a tier unlocks late, its first quote starts the same ramp. The old tier adds one of each Core per five additional purchases after its ceiling while the new tier is locked. Existing active quotes and contributed Cores are preserved.

Topics: Research · Balance · Progression · Exploration · Persistence

**2026-11-08 · Changed · Implemented · Published by 19dc40f**

Newly encountered and legacy camps without a recorded result remain neutral. Current stock is separate from the previous result, including retained Dwarven credit and immediate retry behavior. Timers pause with simulation and saved check results survive reload.
- The first visited, unlocked Dwarven settlement in each factory gets a dry-humor Mythril Tribute modal: Suppressing this one is probably a good idea. It names the actual AP/check and Mythril rate, explains minimum size and first production unlock, and distinguishes Goblin/Dwarven Tribute. Acknowledgement uses saved invitation history, survives undo and excludes tutorial factories; it takes priority over generic tutorial invitations.
- Camp lessons explain 1/3/8/20 bot AP, bar/results, minimum-size Tribute, direct collection from the separate box, selectable Goblin basic resources versus Dwarven Mythril, varying demand/rates and different failed-check consequences. Increase only the isolated practice Cart from five to twenty Small Bots, enough to demonstrate fully suppressed real Tribute. Main-game camp timing, prices, payments and outputs are unchanged.
- The user selects a persistent playtest build guide rather than cross-game factory-template carryover. Preserve verified Simple-through-Hydro wiring, the successful S2 Medium Bot checkpoint and real Road delivery, fuel/Plate repairs, isolated-inventory verification and later failed scaling limitations. Exact successful node positions/graph were not exported, so the guide labels functional recipe reconstructions honestly. AGENTS/browser-startup instructions require consulting it before later runs; new games get no stock or factory grants.

### NODE-0321 · Apply explicit corner quotas and a seven-Core Research Center victory

**2026-11-05 · Changed · Implemented · Published by df69900**

Set Alpha to 50 AP, Foundry to 50 Circuit B/25 Sealed Frames/100 Mythril Gears and Grove to 500 growth; retire Endgame Theory and require manually selected victory research costing one of every Core.

- Explicit user quantities replace five-minute sizing for Alpha, Foundry and Grove. Whole-bot Alpha first payment takes 17 Medium Bots/425 base seconds and preserves one AP surplus; Foundry producer work is 500/450/300 seconds; Grove is 500 base seconds. Reclamation retains ten Fines/66 Flux/36 Crust/22 Slag and proportional mixed credit. Existing progress/overstock remains intact.
- Endgame Theory is absent from live catalogs, browsing, search and selection. Retain its serialized legacy definition for importing historical payments/completions. Retired selections archive contributions and clear safely on load/replace; already completed history is preserved, not refunded or replayed.
- Victory remains an ordinary explicitly selected Research Center project after first Final Core production, with no trigger. It now atomically consumes one each of Simple, Basic, Automata, Hydro, Shiny, Complex and Final Cores. Merely producing/delivering the Final Core never wins. Missing any Core preserves all supplied inputs; completed victory acknowledgement remains saved.
- Required fixed capability/victory research totals 97 Cores: 30 Simple, 29 Basic, 24 Automata, 9 Hydro, 3 Shiny, 1 Complex, 1 Final. Map Node purchases and Basic Cores consumed inside bots are additional. An ideal H route to all corners uses 30 new maps/29 repeat purchases: 692 research Cores with scheduled Automata access, or 562 if Automata is delayed until after exploration. These are geometric/unlock-order examples, not a seed-independent solvability or total factory-consumption claim. Update the reproducible audit and maintain ordinary Wire/Gun suggestions as unimplemented.

<a id="node-0312"></a>
### NODE-0312 · Create, apply and share data mods through guided factory editors

**2026-11-04 · Added · Implemented · Published by eca1c2d**

Options now opens a Mods modal with category editors, base-game templates, local drafts, a change review, save-aware activation and copy/paste NODEMOD1 share codes.

- Guided forms edit existing resources/items, Production nodes including Extractors and Lake Intakes, Logistics/storage including Carts, world obstructions, research, recipes, map fields/reserves and connection/construction timings. Ingredients, yields, chance products, allowed recipes, socket types/counts/sharing, buffers and per-node cable limits are editable without JSON.
- Share codes encode versioned UTF-8 data with a copy-damage checksum. Strict validation rejects unsupported keys, invalid references and quantities, malformed codes and research dependency cycles before applying any changes. Scripts and new runtime behavior are outside the data format.
- Applied packages travel with saves and undo. Catalog activation retains cached research/build references and source-owned base templates; tutorials and clean factories use base rules. Playfield scenes refresh when a package changes. Active stock remains intact when capacities fall, and Cart total-capacity behavior remains enforced.
- Application and removal reject incompatible attached cables atomically. Reserve settings affect newly generated nodes/maps; build and travel settings affect future construction/shipments. Removing a package restores rules without rewinding stock, research or generated maps. Existing milestone triggers, scripted effects/caps and routing roles remain.
- A separate local paused factory verified Options to Mods, a named Wood-to-Timber draft, review/application, generated-code copy/reimport, damaged-code feedback, base-template recovery after development updates and Production-editor controls. Use Complex Core and Complex tier labels in recipes, research, current documentation, planning notes and textual reports while preserving the serialized command-core item and recipe IDs.

### NODE-0234 · Condense Options and shortcut node selection

**2026-11-03 · Changed · Implemented · Local**

Options uses two columns for the four preference toggles and moves button descriptions/flavor to hover help. Shortcut node assignment uses narrow columns of at most five names, expanding toward the roomier screen edge.

Reset shortcut bars uses the same compact presentation.

Use the available modal content height so all four fit at the standard desktop size; narrow screens retain two columns with wrapping labels and omit decorative toggle glyphs.
- The shortcut assignment menu uses 152-pixel icon/name columns with no category subtext and at most five choices vertically. Compare left/right space at the invoking slot, place and order columns toward the roomier side, and clamp the menu to the viewport. Short viewports may use fewer than five rows; horizontal scrolling and keyboard focus reach overflow columns on small screens.

<a id="node-0232"></a>
### NODE-0232 · Show Road flow arrows and move cargo directly without Road storage

**2026-11-03 · Changed · Superseded · Local**

Roads show a large flow arrow, use standard header icons and hold no inventory. Map Road hovers show only direction and item; Cart-family nodes use an icon header and the same shading dimensions as Roads.

- A large filled arrow below the Road header points toward the map edge for export and into the field for import. It derives orientation from the inward-facing socket, supports all four faces, appears in placement previews and updates when pair direction changes. Header symbols match the standard 21-unit node icon in a 33-unit plate.
- Cart, Truck, Train and legacy Chest visuals replace header title text with the existing centered storage icon. Their 42-unit shaded header matches Road geometry. Keep the four-by-five capacity squares, 128-unit footprint, inventory capacities, ports, serialized IDs and control-group markers.
- Minimap and expanded-map Road tooltips contain only a compass route such as H to W1 and the material name. Infer material from both ends, wiring and stock, with the last assigned cable item as a fallback when empty. Remove the Road transport label and verbose map names.
- Each export check moves one item directly from a connected upstream inventory to a connected downstream input, at 12 items per minute. Support local logistics routes on both sides, exact recipe sockets, shared or per-item capacity, real in-flight reservations, single-material and fluid restrictions, valid reciprocal roles, and Dev Source. No receiving or sending Road inventory is filled. Disconnected/full destinations leave cargo at its source, and missed trips never accumulate.
- Local cable animation uses cosmetic pulses and never reserves receiving inventory. Existing save cargo returns to connected inventories if space permits; any remainder is preserved in the shared pool. Pending Road deliveries refund their original sources before migration. Migration clears Road bags, sets capacity to zero and is idempotent; retired Fluid Road cargo retains its prior compatibility handling.
- Retain atomic paired placement, face-slot rules and one-way direction controls from NODE-0228. Direction changes still disconnect endpoint cables for rewiring; stock remains in actual inventories. Expansion dialogue and checks now wait for both Carts to be connected before shipping, rather than treating a Road endpoint as storage.
- NODE-0433 replaces sending-side routing and direct non-vehicle feeds. Road graphics, cargo recovery, and receiving-side routing remain in use.

Topics: Roads · Logistics · Storage · Interface · Tutorials · Persistence · Testing

Supersedes: [NODE-0228](<2026-11.md#node-0228>)

Replaced by: [NODE-0433](<2026-11.md#node-0433>)

Verification: Full Vitest suite: 619 passed, one existing skip. Final TypeScript/Vite build passed. Eight added regressions cover disconnected/full receivers, no catch-up trips, logistics on both sides, capacity reservations versus cosmetic pulses, Dev Source, reversal, pending-delivery refunds and save-load cargo conservation/idempotence. Updated earlier buffer-based Road and tutorial assertions to test direct delivery. <a id="node-0228"></a>
### NODE-0228 · Validate facing Road pairs and toggle one-way flow from either endpoint

**2026-11-03 · Changed · Superseded · Local**

Roads create facing endpoints together only when both placements are legal. Clicking either endpoint opens a compact Road flow panel: click its direction card to reverse the pair, with 5 sec / item underneath.

- Retain atomic validation of both locations and both face-slot limits. Select the nearest intended map face first; if its neighbor is locked, reject placement instead of snapping to a different unlocked face. Valid pairs have reciprocal IDs, facing edges and opposite sending/receiving roles.
- Road clicks and configuration controls open the side panel. Its only content below the title is the clickable direction card and 5 sec / item, per the follow-up request; explanatory subtext, separate Send/Receive cards, material summaries and other text are removed.
- An explicit setRoadDirection operation updates both endpoints to complementary roles; reverseRoad delegates to it. An already-selected direction is a no-op. Reversing preserves stored items and disconnects both endpoint cable sets for rewiring, retaining existing direction-change behavior. No new face slots are used.
- Only export-to-import pairs dispatch across maps. Malformed double-export or mismatched counterpart references cannot dispatch; valid explicit direction selection can repair complementary roles. Legacy omitted reverse references remain compatible.
- Expansion copy explains paired legal placement and one-way flow. Its direction toggle is disabled during practice to preserve the required lesson route; normal play permits reversal from either endpoint.

Topics: Roads · Logistics · Interface · Tutorials · Testing

Replaced by: [NODE-0229](<2026-11.md#node-0229>), [NODE-0232](<2026-11.md#node-0232>)

Verification: Full Vitest suite: 611 passed with one existing skip. Final TypeScript/Vite production build passed. Twelve new Road regressions cover all four faces, side-effect-free previews, atomic local/remote obstruction and remote-capacity failures, locked-neighbor rejection, no fallback to a different face, counterpart identity, stock preservation, explicit direction selection from both ends, unchanged-direction no-op, save/load and single-direction dispatch. Browser QA at port 4175 in a separate paused factory used Dev research/map unlocks, placed a Road and visited its automatically created counterpart, verified opposite roles and control from each map, then rebuilt/reloaded and verified the final single card toggles H to E1 and E1 to H while only 5 sec / item remains below. Main factory and other tabs preserved. This was development QA, not a balance run. Changelog generated and checked. Local only; not published.

<a id="node-0225"></a>
### NODE-0225 · Add Road-unlocked Carts, global Truck and Train upgrades, and shared face slots

**2026-11-03 · Changed · Implemented · Local**

Wooden Chests become Road-unlocked Carts. Sequential Trucks and Trains research upgrades every vehicle to 40 and 80 items without replacement; all solid tiers are allowed from the start. Road faces support one, then two, then three endpoints through sequential upgrades.

- Retain serialized woodenChest/chest node kinds, IDs, wiring, stock, geometry and the existing two-second local output cycle. Normal new placement and copies require Road research; existing vehicles remain usable. Generated saved names and templates update to Cart, Truck or Train, with player-authored titles retained. Build, shortcuts and placement text use the current vehicle name.
- Use capacities of 20 for Cart, 40 for Truck and 80 for Train, shared across input/output stock and pending input reservations. Preserve saved over-capacity stock rather than deleting it; the existing four-by-five block display shows the filled proportion.
- Trucks costs 20 each Simple, Basic and Automata Cores after Road and Automata Core. Trains costs 40 each through Hydro after Trucks and Hydraulic Assembly. Completion upgrades all existing sector nodes and saved templates in place, and future placements start at the current level. Load, state replacement, developer research unlocks and undo reconcile the same level.
- The user explicitly rejected material-tier restrictions: every solid product and Core tier is allowed in the first Cart. New fluid inputs, including Water, Coolant and Conductive Slurry, are blocked in every vehicle level, including routed/legacy connections and Dev Source supply. Existing stored material is preserved.
- Road Channels 1 retains its 2-each-through-Hydro price and now requires Road plus Hydraulic Assembly, adding the second slot per map face. Road Channels 2 retains 130 each through Shiny and requires Road Channels 1 plus Ore Separation, adding the third slot. Both sides of placement count sending and receiving endpoints together. Reversal uses no new slot; saved excess pairs remain operational. Road speed remains one item per five seconds for every material.
- Expansion shows a persistent W1 sending Cart → Road pair → H receiving Cart route diagram, explicitly guides both real connections, and completes only when produced Wood arrives Home. Basics retains its buffer exercise with an explicitly practice-only Cart schematic loan; normal-game unlocks are unchanged by tutorials. Updated current catalog/docs and regenerated live research-consumption analysis for the additional research prices.

Topics: Research · Storage · Logistics · Roads · Tutorials · Persistence · Interface

Supersedes: [NODE-0080](<2026-11.md#node-0080>)

Verification: Full Vitest suite passed 599 tests with one existing skip. Final focused Cart/Road/tutorial checks passed all 26 tests after adding future-placement coverage and refining copy; final TypeScript/Vite build passed. npm run progression passed and refreshed its generated analysis. Browser QA on separate port-4175 preview used a Dev research grant only to open isolated practice, then completed all 11 Expansion steps through normal controls: real Wood production, Road pair placement, sending Cart connection, receiving Cart connection and Achieved state. Verified the upgraded Build catalog says Train, 80 solid items. Main factory at port 4174 and existing public/history tabs preserved. This was development QA, not a balance run; local implementation only, not published. Changelog generation and checks passed.

<a id="node-0211"></a>
### NODE-0211 · Check Research Center Cores immediately on project selection

**2026-11-03 · Changed · Superseded · Local**

Selecting or resuming research immediately consumes one complete buffered Core set when the Research Center is ready, credits project progress and starts the successful-check glow.

- Later payments retain the five-second base interval and all six research-speed ranks. A saved payment deadline prevents same-project clicks, cancellation, switching and save/load from bypassing that interval.
- An incomplete set consumes nothing and starts no glow. Existing waiting-for-material behavior remains after an unsuccessful initial check. Disabled, processing-paused, unfinished or locked-sector Centers do not charge on selection.
- Existing contributions are restored before checking. Only still-required Core types are consumed, so an immediately successful final payment can complete a resumed project. Unavailable project requests preserve current processing and feedback.
- Extends NODE-0159 with an immediate initial check while retaining its interval baseline; successful payments use the glow added in NODE-0207.

Topics: Research · Simulation · Testing · Persistence

Replaced by: [NODE-0413](<2026-11.md#node-0413>)

Verification: Full Vitest suite: 583 passed, 1 existing skip. TypeScript/Vite production build passed. Added immediate-payment, atomic missing-input, ineligible-center, cooldown-through-switch/cancel/save/load, resumed-final-payment and invalid-selection regressions; checked exact subsequent payment boundaries at every speed rank and updated the resumed-project UI expectation. Changelog generation and checks passed. No browser or balance run for this simulation change; no publication.

<a id="node-0207"></a>
### NODE-0207 · Pulse Research Centers between successful Core payments

**2026-11-03 · Changed · Implemented · Local**

Each successful synchronized Core-set payment renews the blue completion flash for the full current research interval, so a continuously supplied Research Center keeps pulsing.

- Start feedback only when the entire required set is consumed, rather than on an individual Core arrival. Use the upgraded cycle duration and repeat the existing 480 ms blue completion pulse until the next check.
- Renew on each success; expire without another payment. Explicit cancellation or project switching clears prior feedback, while the final payment retains its full glow window.
- Timing follows simulation time across global pauses and save/load. Reduced-motion mode uses a steady soft highlight during the same window.

Topics: Research · Interface · Animation · Simulation

Verification: Full Vitest suite: 574 passed and 1 pre-existing skipped test. TypeScript/Vite production build passed. Browser visual check on a separate local preview at http://127.0.0.1:4175/ used Dev Source and developer research unlocks with Storage Connection Capacity: successive payments advanced project progress and the Research Center alternated between illuminated and unlit pulse frames. This was development QA, not a balance run. The live factory at port 4174 was preserved. Local implementation only; not published.

<a id="node-0159"></a>
### NODE-0159 · Use a five-second Research Center baseline before further balance calculations

**2026-11-02 · Fixed · Implemented · Published by eca1c2d**

Apply Q26A: new and loaded Research Centers consume synchronized Core sets every five seconds before upgrades. Existing six research ranks reduce the interval to 4.5, 4, 3.5, 3, 2.5 and 2 seconds. Record Q26 starter-camp support for a modest Shiny factory with expansion room.

- Correct the mismatch between newly placed one-second labs and original-import five-second labs. Normalize aliases and saved templates; preserve stock, existing progress and completed upgrade ranks. Core prices, ingredient recipes and upgrade factors are unchanged.
- Live analysis separates successful-set counts from lab seconds, uses five seconds per base set and sizes fully supplied base-speed factories at that rate. Older analytical experiments are marked requiring revalidation; historical playtest elapsed times are not rescaled.
- Existing pacing goals require renewed assessment; no claim that the revised three-hour or full-endgame targets are met. Q27 next reviews Medium Bot upkeep capacity before choosing Dwarven AP/reward values.

Topics: Research · Progression · Planning · Persistence · Testing

Verification: 568 tests passed, one existing skip; TypeScript and production build passed; npm run progression passed and regenerated live reports. Dev tools can place a movable Dev Source with a multi-connection output that supplies each connected input with its required materials automatically.

- Available through Dev tools rather than the ordinary Build catalog. Placement, movement, deletion, save/load and undo use the existing node controls.
- Each semantic recipe socket gets its own material. A research CORE socket receives all outstanding required Core types. Bots, Stone, Forest Growth and any extant POWER input are inferred from the input family; deferred Power equipment stays deferred.
- An unconfigured production node may be wired to a Dev Source but receives nothing until its recipe is chosen. Recipe changes preserve valid Dev Source cables and update supplied materials.
- Extractors mine a virtual infinite reserve on their normal cycle, inferred from downstream demand. Lake Intakes accept a Dev Source upstream. Generic input families infer demand through local logistics or use an existing material, then Iron as fallback.
- Input buffers respect capacity and pending physical deliveries; cosmetic pulses do not create physical stock. Production time, research prerequisites, camp effects and visible obstruction boundaries remain in force. Ordinary sources keep their compatibility checks.
- Development QA only: these grants are not evidence about normal balance or progression. The live player game was preserved.
- NODE-0433 replaces direct Dev Source connections to sending Roads. Other Dev Source inputs retain their behavior.

Topics: Controls · Interface · Production · Research · Connections · Playtesting

Replaced by: [NODE-0433](<2026-11.md#node-0433>)

Verification: 558 Vitest tests passed, one existing skip, including 12 focused Dev Source tests; TypeScript and Vite build passed. Browser QA at isolated 127.0.0.1:4175 verified Dev tools placement, one source simultaneously feeding an Extractor and both Copper/Charcoal Smelter inputs, and completed Iron extraction/Copper Plate output. No browser console errors in that preview. Changelog generation/checks passed. Local changes only; no publication.

<a id="node-0127"></a>
### NODE-0127 · Use visible obstruction borders and reserve maximum camp growth on spawn

**2026-11-02 · Fixed · Implemented · Local**

Lakes, Deep Holes, camps and Golems share their drawn outline with placement, cable blocking and camp encroachment. Generation validates maximum camp size and reserves its full maximum radius from resources.

- Camp placement validates its 100% visible body against field edges and other obstructions before starting at 25%. Resources reserve the entire maximum circle plus their full body and ordinary placement clearance.
- Runtime encroachment uses whole machine bodies and the union of camp polygons. Resources and other obstructions are protected; shrinking restores clear machines.
- Adaptive cable segments intersect visible outlines without an invisible ellipse halo. Obstructed existing cables stop routing and refund reserved transfers, then resume when clear.
- Generation rules apply to newly generated sectors. Existing saved layouts are preserved.

Topics: World map · Combat · Connections · Resources

Verification: 541 Vitest tests passed, one skipped; TypeScript/Vite build passed. Local changes only; no publication performed. Regression generation covered 1,210 sectors across ten seeds and validated all 300 camps at maximum size, including 150 Dwarven camps. Tests cover shorelines, small holes on long cables, whole-body encroachment and transfer refunds.
